# Data Access Guide

This guide provides a comprehensive overview of data access patterns in a JUDO application. It covers how to inject Data Access Objects (DAOs), perform queries, and use masks for performance optimization.

## Table of Contents
- [Injecting DAOs and Services](#injecting-daos-and-services)
- [Query Patterns](#query-patterns)
- [CRUD Operations](#crud-operations)
- [Using Masks (Projections)](#using-masks-projections)
  - [Why Use Masks?](#why-use-masks)
  - [Mask Basics](#mask-basics)
  - [Common Mask Patterns](#common-mask-patterns)
  - [Performance Comparison](#performance-comparison)

---

## Injecting DAOs and Services

The primary way to interact with the database is by injecting DAOs and other OSGi services into your custom implementation classes using the `@Reference` annotation.

```java
// Standard DAO injection for entities
@Reference
private EntityDao entityDao;

@Reference
private ChildEntityDao childEntityDao;

// Injecting other custom operations
@Reference
private RecalculateRelatedData recalculateRelatedData;

// Injecting JUDO runtime services
@Reference
private VariableResolver variableResolver;

// Injecting an external service (from another OSGi bundle)
@Reference
private ExternalDataService externalDataService;
```

## Query Patterns

The generated DAOs provide a fluent API for building type-safe queries.

```java

// Read by ID with a mask to load specific fields
ParentEntity parentEntity = parentEntityDao.getById(
    parentEntityId,
    ParentEntityMask.parentEntityMask()
        .withValidationErrors(ValidationErrorMask.validationErrorMask())
        .withIsValid()
).orElseThrow();

// Update a full entity
entity.setStatus(false);
entity = entityDao.update(entity);

// Update with a mask (only updates the specified fields)
childEntityDao.update(childEntity, ChildEntityMask.childEntityMask().withIsDefault());

// Delete an entity
entityDao.delete(entity);

## Using Masks (Projections)

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **Masks are the most critical pattern for backend performance.** They allow you to specify exactly which fields and relationships should be loaded or updated, preventing common performance issues like N+1 queries.

### Why Use Masks?

**Without masks:**
- ❌ Loads ALL fields and relationships, leading to N+1 query problems.
- ❌ Slower database queries and higher memory usage.
- ❌ Unnecessary data transfer between the application and the database.

**With masks:

[2691] TOOLRESULT: ## Intent

Keep a simple chronological log of human-readable events on a parent entity. Each event is a History row with three plain values: who acted, what was done, and when. No enumeration of action types is needed.

### Actor and action are plain text

The actor and the action are stored as strings, not as references or enum values. The row stays readable without joins, and the log needs no schema change when a new kind of event appears.

### History is composed by the parent

History rows belong to one parent entity and are removed with it. They are not shared across parents.

## Structure

The History entity is non-CRUD. It has three required attributes: the actor (a string), the action description (a string) and the timestamp of the action. It has no relations of its own.

The parent entity composes History rows through a zero-to-many composition relation.

## Constraints

- The actor, the action and the timestamp are required on every History row.
- History rows are written by backend code, not created by the generated actions.
- The timestamp is set when the event happens and is not changed later.
- The action text is free text, so it cannot be filtered by type. Use an enumeration-based entry instead if filtering by type is needed.

## Variations

- **Enumeration of actions.** Replace the free-text action with an enumeration so events can be filtered and counted. See [[audit-log-entity]].
- **Shared audit table.** Keep one log for all entities instead of a history pe

### Purpose

Customize the hero section (landing page banner) and application logo.

### Hero Customization

The hero (profile header) properties are customized with a `CustomHeroPropsHook` (`CUSTOM_HERO_PROPS_HOOK_INTERFACE_KEY` = `'CustomHeroPropsHook'`, defined in `~/hooks/useHeroProps`). It is a hook factory that returns a function mapping the defaults to new props. `UseHeroProps` has `imgSrc`, `actorName`, `preferredUsername?` and `email?`.

```typescript
context.registerService<CustomHeroPropsHook>(
  CUSTOM_HERO_PROPS_HOOK_INTERFACE_KEY,
  () => (defaults) => ({ ...defaults, imgSrc: '/images/custom-hero.png' }),
);
```

### Logo Customization

A `CustomLogoPropsHook` (`CUSTOM_LOGO_PROPS_HOOK_INTERFACE_KEY` = `'CustomLogoPropsHook'`, in `~/hooks/useLogoProps`) maps the default `UseLogoProps` (`subTitle`, `imgSrc`, `iconSrc`) to custom values. Image paths are relative to `public/`.

```typescript
context.registerService<CustomLogoPropsHook>(
  CUSTOM_LOGO_PROPS_HOOK_INTERFACE_KEY,
  () => (defaults) => ({ ...defaults, imgSrc: '/images/custom-logo.svg', subTitle: '[Your Application]' }),
);
```

The application logo and icon defaults come from the actor's `applicationLogo` in the model; prefer changing the model for a permanent change.

### Custom Hero Component

The hero has no component-level hook, only the props hook above. Replacing the hero component requires a template override (see [Template Override Process](../README.md#template-override-process)).

// G E N E R A T E D    S O U R C E

import { OBJECTCLASS } from '@pandino/pandino-api';
import { useTrackService } from '@pandino/react-hooks';
import { getUser } from '../auth';

export const CUSTOM_HERO_PROPS_HOOK_INTERFACE_KEY = 'CustomHeroPropsHook';
export type CustomHeroPropsHook = () => (defaults: UseHeroProps) => UseHeroProps;

export interface UseHeroProps {
  imgSrc: string;
  actorName: string;
  preferredUsername?: string;
  email?: string;
}

export function useHeroProps(): UseHeroProps {
  const { profile } = getUser();
  const defaults: UseHeroProps = {
    imgSrc: 'ninja.png',
    actorName: 'Customer',
    preferredUsername: profile?.preferred_username,
    email: profile?.email,
  };
  const { service: useCustomHeroProps } = useTrackService<CustomHeroPropsHook>(
    `(${OBJECTCLASS}=${CUSTOM_HERO_PROPS_HOOK_INTERFACE_KEY})`,
  );
  const getCustomHeroProps = useCustomHeroProps && useCustomHeroProps();

  return getCustomHeroProps ? getCustomHeroProps(defaults) : defaults;
}

## Intent

An entity exposes two explicit, complementary user actions: a guarded hard `delete` and a Boolean `active` with a `toggleActive` operation. The delete runs only when the entity is not referenced by audit-critical data. If it is referenced, the operation raises a modeled business fault (HTTP 422, so the reason reaches the UI) and deletes nothing; the user retires the record with `toggleActive` instead. Unlike [[guard-clause-delete]], which returns silently on a blocked delete, this fails loudly so the frontend can tell the user why and steer them to deactivate.

## Practice

### Platform base

- The generated DAO of a mapped, non-abstract type offers `getById`, `existsById`, `delete` and per-relation `query...()` accessors ([generated DAO behaviour](../../agent-docs/backend/data-access-guide.md#which-types-get-a-full-dao)).
- A refusal the user must see is a modeled fault declared on the operation, thrown as the generated checked exception; it reaches the client as 422 with `X-Fault` ([modeled faults](../../agent-docs/backend/error-handling-guide.md#modeled-faults), [[business-error-vs-generic-operation-error]]).
- A custom-implemented operation is not intercepted: interceptors fire only for the built-in create/update/delete/reference operations ([which operations are intercepted](../../agent-docs/backend/interceptors.md#which-operations-are-intercepted)). The guard therefore has to live in the implementation itsel
[2732] TOOLRESULT: fragment: "none: frontend-only"
first_seen: 2026-03-04
last_updated: 2026-11-10
---
## Intent

A form field should be mandatory only when another field has a certain value (for example a name that is required only for active records). The rule lives in a frontend action hook, so the generated page stays untouched.

## Practice

### Platform base

- The generator emits per-page and per-container hook points. A page hook has the key `<PAGE>_ACTIONS_HOOK_INTERFACE_KEY` and the type `<Container>ActionsHook`, both exported from the page's generated `customization` module (dialogs: `~/dialogs/<path>/customization`). Container hooks use `<CONTAINER>_CONTAINER_ACTIONS_HOOK_INTERFACE_KEY` from `~/containers/<path>/customization` ([action hooks](../../agent-docs/frontend/hooks/action-hooks.md)).
- The hook is a function that receives the page data (`data`, `editMode`, `storeDiff`, depending on the page kind) and returns the handlers. The object you return must match the generated action type, so read the generated `customization.ts` and `types.ts` for the available handler names; the handler names in the docs are illustrative ([validation hooks](../../agent-docs/frontend/hooks/validation-hooks.md)).
- Scaffolds are `.default` files under `src/custom/hooks/{pages,dialogs,containers}/`. Copy one to the name without `.default`, implement it and call its `register...Hook(context)` from `src/custom/application-customizer.tsx` ([using .default scaffolds](../../agent-docs/frontend/hooks/READM
[2733] TOOLRESULT: fragment: "none: backend-only"
first_seen: 2026-11-10
last_updated: 2026-11-10
---
## Intent

Who may write what is a security rule, and a rule written as a free-form interceptor per entity is easy to leave incomplete: a write that nobody lists is simply unguarded, with no error and no log line. This practice states the rule as a small number of guards, each one a set of write operations plus the roles allowed to perform them, and adds a test that derives the full set of write operations from the model and fails when any has no guard. A second level, a central guard service, handles what a role list cannot: ownership and state of the row being written.

## Practice

### Platform base

- A write guard is an `OperationCallInterceptor` registered as an OSGi component with `judo.model.name=<app>`; `getOperations(AsmModel)` returns the operations it covers, and an empty collection means all built-in operations ([interceptor contract](../../agent-docs/backend/interceptors.md#the-interceptor-contract)).
- Interceptors fire **only for built-in behaviour operations** (create, update, delete, set/unset/add/remove reference and so on). Custom, mapped and script operations never reach them, so guards for those go inside each `*CustomImplementation` ([which operations are intercepted](../../agent-docs/backend/interceptors.md#which-operations-are-intercepted), [custom operations](../../agent-docs/backend/custom-operations.md)).
- Operation names are fully qualified, for example `<app>.<pac

// After a successful checkout the new order opens: the newest order of the customer is the one just placed.

const checkoutHook: CheckoutInputCheckoutInput_FormActionsHook = () => {
  const { navigate } = useJudoNavigation();
  return {
    postCheckoutForCartInfoAction: async (onSubmit) => {
      await onSubmit();
      const service = new CustomerServiceForOrdersImpl(judoAxiosProvider);
      const response = await service.list(undefined, {
        _mask: '{number}',
        _orderBy: [{ attribute: 'orderedAt', descending: true }],
      } as any);
      const newest = response.data[0];
      if (newest) {
        navigate(routeToCustomerOrdersAccessViewPage(newest.__signedIdentifier));
      }
    },
  };
};

[2754] TOOLRESULT: ## Overview

`OperationCallInterceptor` is a powerful mechanism for implementing cross-cutting concerns that apply to business logic operations. They allow you to execute custom code **before** (`preCall`) and **after** (`postCall`) a modeled operation is invoked.

Common use cases include:
- **Auditing**: Creating a log of who performed what action and when.
- **Payload Manipulation**: Modifying input data before an operation or transforming the result after.
- **Side Effects**: Triggering other operations or external service calls in response to an event.
- **Complex Validation**: Enforcing business rules that involve multiple entities or external state.

### OperationCallInterceptor Interface

JUDO provides the `OperationCallInterceptor` interface for intercepting model operations:

```java
public interface OperationCallInterceptor {
    String getName();
    default Collection<EOperation> getOperations(AsmModel asmModel) { return Collections.emptyList(); }
    default boolean async()                { return false; }
    default boolean terminateOnException() { return true; }
    default boolean ignoreDecoratedCall()  { return false; }
    default Object preCall(EOperation operation, Object parameterPayload)
            throws InterceptorCallBusinessException { return parameterPayload; }
    default Object postCall(EOperation operation, Object parameterPayload, Object returnPayload)
            throws InterceptorCallBusinessException { return returnPayload; }
}
```

### Th
[2755] TOOLRESULT: ## Example 4: Pre-Call Payload Manipulation

**Pattern**: Modify input payload before operation execution

### Pre-Call Interceptor Code

```java
@Component(property = { "judo.model.name=<app>" })
public class ParentEntityCreateInterceptor implements OperationCallInterceptor {

    @Reference ChildEntityDao childEntityDao;
    @Reference ParentEntityDao parentEntityDao;
    @Reference LocationService locationService;

    private AsmUtils asmUtils;
    private List<EOperation> interceptedOperations;

    @Override
    public String getName() {
        return this.getClass().getSimpleName();
    }

    @Override
    public Collection<EOperation> getOperations(AsmModel asmModel) {
        if (interceptedOperations == null) {
            if (asmUtils == null) {
                asmUtils = new AsmUtils(asmModel.getResourceSet());
            }

            EOperation parentEntityCreateOperation =
                    asmUtils.resolveOperation("<app>.services.ServiceContext#_createInstanceParentEntities")
                            .orElseThrow();

            interceptedOperations = List.of(parentEntityCreateOperation);
        }
        return interceptedOperations;
    }

    @Override
    public Object preCall(EOperation operation, Object parameterPayload) {
        Payload input = ((CreateInstanceCallPayload) parameterPayload).getInput();

        // Validate input before operation
        // getAs returns null when the key is absent: compare with Boolean.TRUE instead of unbox
[2756] TOOLRESULT: ## Reading the Principal and Actor in an Interceptor

The dispatcher keeps request context in an exchange map. The key constants are defined on `hu.blackbelt.judo.dispatcher.api.Dispatcher`: `PRINCIPAL_KEY` (`"__principal"`), `ACTOR_KEY` (`"__actor"`), `INSTANCE_KEY_OF_BOUND_OPERATION` (`"__this"`) and `ENTITY_TYPE_MAP_KEY` (`"__entityType"`). The signed identifier of an instance is read from the `__signedIdentifier` payload key. Actor and principal variables are also available to JQL and Java through `VariableResolver.resolve(String.class, "ACTOR", "identifier")` (see [Custom Operations](custom-operations.md)).

## Operation FQN Format Reference

Operation Fully Qualified Names follow specific patterns:

| Operation Type | FQN Pattern | Example |
|----------------|-------------|---------|
| Create (Transfer) | `[model_name].[actor_package].[ActorName]#_createInstance[EntityPlural]` | `<app>.entity_admin.Admin#_createInstanceEntities` |
| Create (Contained) | `[model_name].[entity_package].[Parent]#_createInstance[ChildEntityPlural]` | `<app>.data_management.DataGroup#_createInstanceDataItems` |
| Update | `[model_name].[entity_package].[Entity]#_updateInstance[FullEntityFQN]` | `<app>.access_management.AccessRole#_updateInstance<app>_access_management_AccessRole` |
| Delete | `[model_name].[entity_package].[Entity]#_deleteInstance[FullEntityFQN]` | `<app>.access_management.AccessRole#_deleteInstance<app>_access_management_AccessRole` |
| Add Reference | `[model_name].[entity

## Intent

A client of the generated API can send its own filter with a list call. If an actor may list a type only by one key (for example the tests of a given id), the server must refuse any other filter, otherwise the actor can enumerate everything the list operation can reach. A pre-call interceptor validates the filter before the platform runs the query.

## Practice

### Platform: what is available

- List and query calls are built-in operations, so a `preCall` interceptor registered for the exact list operation fires before the query. Custom, mapped and script operations never reach interceptors ([which operations are intercepted](../../agent-docs/backend/interceptors.md#which-operations-are-intercepted)).
- The list call payload carries the `QueryCustomizer`; its `filter` is a JQL expression string from the client ([low-level DAO and QueryCustomizer](../../agent-docs/backend/data-access-guide.md#low-level-dao-and-querycustomizer)). `preCall` returns the payload (possibly changed); the returned object must be assignable to the expected payload type ([contract](../../agent-docs/backend/interceptors.md#the-interceptor-contract)).
- Refuse with `InterceptorCallBusinessException`: the dispatcher turns it into a 422 with `X-Fault` and the exception's `errorCode`. A raw `RuntimeException` becomes a generic 500 ([business error](../../agent-docs/backend/interceptors.md#throwing-a-business-erro
[2761] TOOLRESULT: fragment: "none: backend-only"
first_seen: 2026-03-04
last_updated: 2026-11-10

## Intent

Options for a relation field (a dropdown or autocomplete) often depend on another value on the same form: only the error codes of the chosen element, only free parking slots for the chosen days, only non-archived cars of the current user. A range interceptor replaces the options the platform returns with a query that uses the current form context.

## Practice

### Platform: what a range interceptor sees

- Range and choice lookups are generated operations (`getRangeFor<Name>` at the REST level, `_getRangeReference<Relation>` as the interceptable operation) whose input carries the `owner` instance and, for mapped references, a `queryCustomizer` ([range operations](../../agent-docs/backend/platform-behavior-reference.md#range-and-choice-operations)). The REST verb is `~range`; the `X-Judo-MarkSelectedRangeItems` header marks already attached items.
- Range interceptors fire **only** on the `_getRangeReference*` operations (reference range), not on plain list or query operations such as `~list` ([which operations are intercepted](../../agent-docs/backend/interceptors.md#which-operations-are-intercepted)).
- Values outside the allowed range are rejected with `NOT_ACCEPTED_BY_RANGE` (same reference). Choosing an option the interceptor hid therefore also fails on save.
- Security: for a relation whose range type exposes every instance (`rangeType=ANY`) a read-only attribute does not stop

## Intent

Restrict what an actor can reach by giving its access a `DERIVED` type and a getter expression, so visibility is enforced in the model.

## Practice

- An actor access has `accessType` `ALL` or `DERIVED`. `ALL` is fixed to `0..*` and has no getter; `DERIVED` has a configurable cardinality (`0..1` or `0..*`) and a getter ([accesspoint.md](../../agent-docs/model/esm_metamodel/accesspoint.md), *AccessType Behaviour*).
- Getter rules, all at once ([architectural patterns](../../agent-docs/backend/architectural-patterns.md), *Rules for `accesses[*].getterExpression`*):
  - the filter starts from the entity, not from the transfer object
  - `self` is not in scope inside an actor access getter; iterate from the entity with a lambda variable
  - a `DERIVED` access needs an expression that returns references; for "see everything" use `ALL` with no getter
  - `!getVariable(...)` returns a scalar; compare with `==`, and qualify the primitive type
- Typical shapes:
  - status filter: `Contest!filter(c | c.status == ContestStatus#OPEN)`
  - singleton: an expression ending in `!any()` with cardinality `0..1`
  - ownership: compare an entity field to the actor's own value, for example `!getVariable("ACTOR", "email")`
  - role-scoped: one `!filter` whose lambda disjuncts a role flag and the ownership clause; JQL has no if/else ([advanced modeling patterns](../../

194-Besides `PRINCIPAL`, `USER` and `ACTOR`, the engine knows more categories, mostly used implicitly:
195-
196:- **`SYSTEM`**: `current_date`, `current_timestamp`, `current_time`, reached through `<Type>!now()` (see *Temporal functions*).
197-- **`PARAMETER`**: the attributes of an input parameter transfer object of a parameterized query become `PARAMETER`-category variables while the expression is resolved.
198-- **`ENVIRONMENT`, `SEQUENCE`, `REQUEST`**: runtime providers registered by the default variable resolver (environment configuration values, database sequences, per-request parameters). An undefined variable resolves to `null` with a warning.
199-
200-`!getVariable` is typed by the primitive it is called on (`Integer`, `Decimal`, `Boolean`, `String`, an enumeration, a custom type, `Date`, `Timestamp`, `Time`); for measured numerics the variable carries the unit. It always returns a scalar, never a collection.
201-
202-### JQL grammar quirks
203-
204-- The ternary `cond ? then : else` is right-associative and compiles to a typed switch expression. The condition must be a logical expression; `then` and `else` must be of the same kind (a decimal result is chosen when either side is decimal; entity branches resolve to a common supertype, and an ambiguous or missing common supertype fails the build).
205-- Equality is `==` and `!=` only (no `=` and no `<>`). Keywords are case-insensitive.

[2774] TOOLRESULT: ### Guard against repeated runs

Choose one guard and document it:

- check whether the seed data already exists before writing it,
- set the `createdAt` marker last, after every write has succeeded, and return early when it is set,
- or keep a counter and run only the numbered steps that are not yet recorded.

### Keep the seed inside one transaction

Write all seed rows in the operation's transaction. A failure then rolls back the whole seed, and the next start retries it cleanly.

### Use project services for lookups

Seed code that looks up existing rows usually calls project services. Those services are project code, not platform API, so copy the pattern and not the names.

## Examples

### mlszksz-platform

- The init operation seeds cities, postal codes, capabilities, the platform configuration singleton, and an admin organization with an admin user.
- It runs with the `createdAt` marker set last.

### doors-model

- The init operation runs numbered migration steps in order. A counter attribute records the last completed step, so each step runs once across deployments.
- A gap in the step numbers marks a step that was removed.

## Intent

Give the application one well-known place where reference data is seeded. A static operation named `init` on a dedicated entity is called once per deployment (by the project's startup or deployment routine) and fills the tables that the rest of the model depends on. The entity itself holds no business data and is not edited through the generated actions.

### Initializer is non-CRUD

The entity turns off generated create, update and delete. Only the init operation changes data, so no user can create a second initializer or edit the marker.

### Init is idempotent or guarded

The operation must be safe to run on every start. Either it checks whether the seed data already exists, or it uses a marker that records a completed run.

## Structure

The Initializer entity has a marker attribute and a single static operation. The marker is either a required timestamp (`createdAt`), a required boolean, or absent. The `init` operation is a static, stateful operation flagged as an initializer, with a custom implementation or a model-defined body. Variants split the work into several named static operations (for example `initUsers` and `initVendors`), or put the operation directly on a domain entity when no separate entity is wanted.

## Constraints

- The entity is non-CRUD: createable, updateable and deleteable are all false in the common form.
- The init operation is static and marked as an initializer, and it is stateful because it writes da

[2788] TOOLRESULT: 
Create the rows of a parent's contained collection (answers of a test, addresses of a partner, states of a workflow version) through the parent's DAO, so the link to the parent is part of the call.

## Practice

### Platform: per-relation create methods

- The generated DAO of a mapped, non-abstract type has per-relation `create`, `set`, `unset`, `add`, `remove` and range methods, subject to the relation's allowed-operation flags (the create method exists only if the relation allows creation) ([which types get a full DAO](../../agent-docs/backend/data-access-guide.md#which-types-get-a-full-dao)). The method for a relation takes the parent entity and the child's `<Child>ForCreate` payload (or a list) and returns the persisted child; the exact names follow the relation names, so read them from the generated DAO interface.
- Child payloads are built with the generated builder ([[builder-pattern-entity-creation]]). A single-valued relation setter in a builder needs a real object, never `null`; clearing is `unset<Relation>` ([clearing a relation](../../agent-docs/backend/data-access-guide.md#clearing-a-relation-unset-not-setnull)).
- The same parent DAO has query methods for the relation. Multi-valued ones return a customizer (`filterBy...`, `maskedBy`, `selectList()`), single-valued ones return the entity directly, so adding `.selectOne()` does not compile ([relationship navigation](../../agent-docs/backend/data-access-guide.md#query-result-methods)).
- Interceptors fire only fo

[2881] TOOLRESULT: import type { BundleContext } from '@pandino/pandino-api';
import { useSnacks } from '~/hooks/useSnacks';
import { ERROR_HANDLER_INTERCEPTOR_INTERFACE_KEY, type ErrorHandlerInterceptorHook } from '~/utilities/error-handling';

// Business faults (HTTP 422) carry a readable `message` (the ShopFault of the model). The generated handler opens
// the fault dialog from the FIRST key of the response body, which here is the plain error code, so the dialog lists
// the code letter by letter (GAPS.md R8-6). Documented route: an error handler interceptor that shows the message
// as a snackbar (agent-docs/frontend/error-handling.md "Customizing the 422 Handling").
const isBusinessFault = (error: any) => error?.response?.status === 422;

const businessMessage = (error: any): string => {
  const body = error?.response?.data ?? {};
  const detail = Object.values(body).find((v: any) => v && typeof v === 'object' && 'message' in (v as object)) as
    | { message?: string }
    | undefined;
  return detail?.message ?? 'The action could not be completed.';
};

const hook: ErrorHandlerInterceptorHook = () => {
  const { showErrorSnack } = useSnacks();
  return {
    shouldInterceptError: (error: any) => isBusinessFault(error),
    interceptError: (error: any) => {
      showErrorSnack(businessMessage(error), { autoHideDuration: 8000 });
    },
  };
};

export function registerErrorHandlerInterceptorHook(context: BundleContext) {
  context.registerService<ErrorHandlerInterceptorHook>(ERROR_HAND

export async function login(page: Page, user = 'admin') {
  await apiHelper.gotoWithWait(page); // app start URL, redirects to Keycloak
  await expect(page.locator('#kc-form-login')).toBeVisible({ timeout: 15000 });
  await page.locator('#username').fill(user);
  await page.locator('#password').fill(user);
  await page.locator('#kc-login').click();

}

export async function openProductList(page: Page) {
  await login(page);
  await navigateToAccess(page, 'Products');
  await expect(tableHelper.getRows(page).first()).toBeVisible();
}

export async function goToMenu(page: Page, label: 'Products' | 'Cart' | 'My orders' | 'My profile' | 'My addresses') {

// A second browser window on the back office, for checks that need an admin to change data (F15).
export async function adminPage(browser: Browser) {
  const context = await browser.newContext({ baseURL: ADMIN_URL, viewport: { width: 1440, height: 900 } });
  const page = await context.newPage();
  await page.goto(ADMIN_URL); // app start URL, redirects to Keycloak
  await expect(page.locator('#kc-form-login')).toBeVisible({ timeout: 15000 });
  await page.locator('#username').fill('admin');
  await page.locator('#password').fill('admin');
  await page.locator('#kc-login').click();
  await expect(page.getByRole('button', { name: 'Products' }).first()).toBeVisible({ timeout: 30000 });
  return { page, context };
}

[2925] TOOLRESULT: 77-### When No Manual Step Is Needed
78-
79:Adding a **stored** member (for example a new attribute) to an entity needs **no** manual migration or `schema-upgrade` run for a fresh or development database: the generated Liquibase changelog (`application/model/target/generated-resources/model/<app>-liquibase_postgresql.changelog.xml`) already contains the new column. A manual schema-upgrade step is only relevant for a persistent database that already holds data, and for genuinely non-additive or backward-incompatible changes (renames, type changes, removals). For a local persistent PostgreSQL database `./judo.sh schema-upgrade` generates the difference against the running database and applies it.
80-
81-**⚠️ IMPORTANT: When Schema Migration is Required**
82-
83-Schema migration is **ONLY necessary when entity types change** in the model. The JUDO platform generates SQL schema from entity type definitions.
84-
85-**Schema migration IS required when:**
86-- ✅ Adding/removing/modifying entity types (mapped entities)
87-- ✅ Adding/removing attributes on entity types

}

Operations of an action group that take an input transfer object or return an output transfer object need UI for both. This practice gives every such operation a dedicated input page and, when the operation returns data, a dedicated output page that can itself offer further operations.

## Practice

### Platform base

- The generator already produces UI for an operation with input and output: an operation button on the view or table, an input form dialog when an input is declared, and a result display when an output is declared ([ESM operations to UI actions](../../agent-docs/frontend/model-screen-layout.md)). Use that default unless the UX needs more.
- An unbound operation can be marked as a template provider (`GET_TEMPLATE`); it returns a prepopulated prototype of the input transfer object, served by the REST layer under `<TO>/~template` ([operation reference](../../agent-docs/model/esm_metamodel/operation.md)). An input page can load this prototype on mount.
- Bound operations are called with the instance's signed identifier, which the server issues in responses and which changes after every update of the entity. Never store or rebuild it; take it from the latest response ([signed identifiers](../../agent-docs/backend/platform-behavior-reference.md)).
- Custom routes are added in `src/extra-routes.tsx`; the generated `routes.tsx` prepends them ([extra routes](../../agent-docs/frontend/extension-points.md)).

### Project practice: dedicated input and output rout

import { login, card, apiHelper } from './helpers-customer';

// R8-2: the customer's product transfer object (ShopProductInfo) has no admin-only fields. A customer browses the
// shop like a user; the test only watches the answers the server sends to that browser.
test.describe('Customer API answers carry no admin-only product data', () => {
  test('the product list and the product details contain no stock number and no active flag', async ({ page }) => {
    const bodies: string[] = [];
    page.on('response', async (r) => {
      if (r.url().includes('/api/webshop/Customer/') && r.request().method() === 'POST' && /~(list|get)$/.test(r.url())) {
        try {
          bodies.push(await r.text());
        } catch {
          /* redirected or empty */
        }
      }
    });
    await login(page);
    await apiHelper.triggerAndWaitWithRegex(page, card(page, 'Running shoe'), /.*~get$/);
    await expect(page.getByRole('textbox', { name: /Stock status/ })).toHaveValue('In stock');
    expect(bodies.some((b) => b.includes('Running shoe'))).toBe(true); // the product list answer
    expect(bodies.length).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(2); // the list and the details page answers
    for (const body of bodies) {
      expect(body).not.toMatch(/"stock"/);
      expect(body).not.toMatch(/"active"/);
      expect(body).not.toMatch(/"variants"/);
    }
  });
});

## Practice

### Platform base and what is project practice

The agent-docs cover only the surroundings: SMTP is configured through `JUDO_PLATFORM_MAIL_SMTP_*` environment values ([email configuration](../../agent-docs/deployment/application-config.md)), the FileStore is a platform service ([[filestore-mediated-file-transfer]]), and custom services are OSGi components. The agent-docs do not describe a Handlebars library or an email-sending API: rendering with Handlebars and the `EmailService` calls below are what the example projects use. Verify the library version and the service API in your project.

### Compile from a stored or inline template

- Load the template text from the FileStore by id (seeded at start-up, see [[init-data-seeding]]) or keep it inline in code.
- Compile it at runtime and apply a context object (a map or a small POJO).
- Hand the rendered text to the mail service, with images attached as inline content, or use the text for any other output.

```java
// Example project code (Handlebars Java library; EmailService is project-used API)
Handlebars handlebars = new Handlebars();
String templateContent = convertStreamToString(fileStoreService.get(templateId));
Template template = handlebars.compileInline(templateContent);

String htmlBody = template.apply(Context.newContext(contextObject));

emailService.sendMessage(EmailService.EmailMessage.emailBuilder()
    .from(senderEmail).to(recipientEmail).subject(subject)
    .htmlTemplate(htmlBody)
    .inputStre

## Intent

Produce PDF and DOCX documents (offers, reports, certificates) from templates that business users maintain as Word files. The pipeline is backend code; the model only needs a place to keep the templates and the generated result.

### Templates are data, not code

Each document type has a DOCX template with Freemarker placeholders. Templates are stored as binary attributes on a singleton configuration entity, so a template can be replaced without a deployment.

## Structure

The pipeline is a chain of services behind one orchestrator, called from cu

### Platform base

[3006] TOOLRESULT: 
Customize the `karaf-features` module and other generated Maven modules (extra bundles, extra repositories, extra plugins or dependencies, OSGi configuration) without editing the generated `feature.xml` or `pom.xml`. The generator emits those files with named override slots; a fragment file you create is spliced in at the matching marker, so the change survives regeneration. This is the backend analog of the frontend patterns [[generator-override-extra-dependencies]], [[handlebars-generator-temp

## Practice

### Platform base

- Generated files such as `pom.xml` and `feature.xml` carry paired marker comments, for example `<!-- To define create 'app/pom.xml.extra-dependencies.fragment.hbs' file -->` and `<!-- End of ... -->`. By default the fragment is an empty stub from the template jar; a file with the same relative path and name in the override directory replaces it ([extending generated files via fragment overrides](../../agent-docs/deployment/build-process.md#extending-generated-files-via-fragment-overrides)).
- Override directory: `application/generator-overrides/`, wired into the generator invocation of the root `pom.xml`. A second root `generator-overrides/` serves the project-root generator step.
- A fragment survives regeneration and needs no `.generator-ignore` entry. The docs say to prefer fragments over `.generator-ignore`: a fragment keeps the file under generator control, an ignore entry freezes it and lets it drift from the template ([available extension poin

[3015] CALL ctx_execute: {"language": "shell", "code": "cd ~/Projects/judo-blueprint/catalog; sed -n 18,75p model-blueprints/platform-configuration-singleton/BLUEPRINT.md | cut -c1-600; echo ======; ls model-blueprints/platform-configuration-singleton; sed -n 1,50p model-blueprints/platform-configuration-singleton/backend.md | cut -c1-500"}
[3016] TOOLRESULT: ## Intent

Keep every platform-wide setting in one place: the sender address, the base URL, expiry durations and the text of notification templates. Exactly one Configuration row exists. It is seeded when the application starts and afterwards only changed, so the rest of the model can read settings from a known location.

### Exactly one configuration row

The entity is not createable and not deleteable. The single row is created by an initializer or seed step (see [[initializer-entity-with-init-operation]]). Updates change the existing row.

### Settings are read from the singleton

Other entities and services read settings from the one row. They do not keep copies, so a change to the sender address or an expiry is seen everywhere on the next read.

## Structure

The Configuration entity holds plain attributes: text for the sender address, base URL and platform name, integers for expiry durations, and long text for email templates. Variants add numeric business parameters such as a margin percentage, a closing time, or binary document templates. Some variants add a composition to a per-currency or per-item sub-entity. The entity is exposed to the user through a transfer object or an input transfer object that maps onto it. Required attributes are the ones the platform cannot run without; template attributes are often optional.

## Constraints

- Createable and deleteable are false. The row must exist before the first read, so seeding is required.
- Updateable is true when a

[3020] TOOLRESULT: 
A fresh deployment needs lookup rows, default configuration and a first account before anyone can use it. An init operation creates them. It runs with no input and no user, and it can be run again without duplicating data.

## Practice

### Platform base

The model defines an initializer operation kind: it must be static and take no input ([operation metamodel](../../agent-docs/model/esm_metamodel/operation.md)). The generated custom-operation class is implemented as an OSGi component with the generated DAOs injected by `@Reference` ([custom operations](../../agent-docs/backend/custom-operations.md)); the docs show an `Init` implementation that delegates to a plain importer class. Custom operations are not wrapped by interceptors ([interceptors](../../agent-docs/backend/interceptors.md)). The agent-docs do not describe a startup hook that calls the operation: how and when it runs is a project convention (see [[initializer-entity-with-init-ope

### Project practice: seed through DAOs, guard against repeats

- Put the init operation on a dedicated entity or a singleton entity. It takes no input and returns nothing.
- Simple data sets: a model script body creates the rows. Larger or conditional sets: Java with the generated DAOs ([[excel-data-seed-import]] for spreadsheet-driven seeding).
- Make it repeatable: check before creating (for example an existence check, an emptiness check on the entity, or a counter of completed migration steps). Without a guard a second run creates 
[3021] CALL ctx_execute: {"language": "shell", "code": "cd ~/Projects/judo-blueprint/catalog; sed -n 18,90p best-practices/backend/injectable-business-clock.md | cut -c1-700"}
[3022] TOOLRESULT: ## Intent

Business rules depend on "now": the next working day, a time window, an expiry. If each class reads `LocalDate.now()` itself, tests and demos cannot move time and different classes can disagree on the time zone. Keep one clock in one place, read it everywhere, and allow a start-up override so a test or demo can run at a chosen date.

## Practice

### Platform base

- `Timestamp!now()` in model expressions follows the platform parameter `defaultZoneId`, while `LocalDateTime.now()` in custom Java follows the JVM time zone ([Time and Time Zones](../../agent-docs/backend/platform-behavior-reference.md#time-and-time-zones)). A single clock for custom Java removes that second source of divergence in Java code, but not in model expressions.
- Custom operations are OSGi components that reach services with `@Reference` ([custom operations](../../agent-docs/backend/custom-operations.md#osgi-component-registration-pattern)). Interceptors are also components ([the interceptor contract](../../agent-docs/backend/interceptors.md#the-interceptor-contract)).

### Project practice: one clock, one override

The classes below are example project code, not platform API.

1. A clock holder: a final class with static accessors (`today()`, `now()`, `nowUtc()`, `instant()`, `get()` and a UTC variant). It has no OSGi dependency, so common services, custom operations and interceptors all use it the same way. An injected `java.time.Clock` field is the alternative when the caller is a componen

title: Frontend Internationalization
description: react-i18next setup, flat translation keys, generated translation files, language switching and custom translation providers.
---

# Internationalization (i18n)

## Overview

The frontend uses **react-i18next** for internationalization. Translation files are located in the `public/i18n/` directory and support multiple languages.

## Directory Structure

**IMPORTANT**: JUDO generates translation files in a flat structure, NOT in subdirectories.

```
public/i18n/
├── application_default.json   # Generated application translations (fallback)
├── application_en-US.json     # Generated application translations (English)
├── application_hu-HU.json     # Generated application translations (Hungarian)
├── system_default.json        # JUDO system translations (fallback)
├── system_en-US.json          # JUDO system translations (English)
└── system_hu-HU.json          # JUDO system translations (Hungarian)
```

**Key Points**:
- ❌ **NOT** organized in subdirectories like `en-US/translation.json`
- ✅ **YES** flat structure with `{type}_{locale}.json` naming
- **application_*.json** - Generated from your model (entities, enumerations, menu, services)
- **system_*.json** - JUDO platform UI translations (buttons, modals, table controls)
- **_default.json** files serve as fallback when specific locale not found

## Translation File Format

### Structure

**JUDO uses flat dot-notation keys**, not neste

[3028] TOOLRESULT: ## Language Switching

### Get Current Language

```typescript
import { useTranslation } from 'react-i18next';

export const LanguageInfo = () => {
  const { i18n } = useTranslation();
  
  return (
    <div>
      Current language: {i18n.language}
    </div>
  );
};
```

### Change Language

```typescript
import { useTranslation } from 'react-i18next';
import { Button } from '@mui/material';

export const LanguageSelector = () => {
  const { i18n } = useTranslation();
  
  const changeLanguage = (lang: string) => {
    i18n.changeLanguage(lang);
  };
  
  return (
    <div>
      <Button onClick={() => changeLanguage('en-US')}>English</Button>
      <Button onClick={() => changeLanguage('hu-HU')}>Magyar</Button>
    </div>
  );
};
```

### Language Dropdown

```typescript
import { useTranslation } from 'react-i18next';
import { Select, MenuItem } from '@mui/material';

export const LanguageDropdown = () => {
  const { i18n } = useTranslation();
  
  const handleChange = (event) => {
    i18n.changeLanguage(event.target.value);
  };
  
  return (
    <Select value={i18n.language} onChange={handleChange}>
      <MenuItem value="en-US">English (US)</MenuItem>
      <MenuItem value="hu-HU">Magyar</MenuItem>
    </Select>
  );
};
```

// src/custom/application-customizer.tsx
import { LanguageDropdown } from './components/LanguageDropdown';

export const applicationCustomizer: ApplicationCustomizer = {
  appBarComponents: [LanguageDropdown],

[3032] TOOLRESULT: ## Intent

Deliver the whole UI in Hungarian: entity labels, menus and enumerations (application texts) as well as login, actions, dialogs and errors (system texts).

## Practice

### Platform base

- Translations are flat files in `public/i18n/`: `application_<locale>.json` (generated from the model) and `system_<locale>.json` (platform UI), plus `*_default.json` fallbacks. Keys are flat dot-notation under a `translation` root ([directory structure](../../agent-docs/frontend/i18n.md)).
- Generated key patterns: `entities.<Entity>.<Component>.<field>`, `enumerations.<Type>.<VALUE>` and `enumerations.<Type>._null`, `menuTree.<label>`, `services.<service>...`, `faults.<prefix>.<object>.<attribute>`, `extra.localization.operation.<name>` ([key conventions](../../agent-docs/frontend/i18n.md)).
- Every `application_<locale>.json`, including `application_hu-HU.json`, is generated from the model. A hand-edited one is overwritten (or fails the checksum check) unless it is in `.generator-ignore`; once ignored it no longer follows the model ([best practices](../../agent-docs/frontend/i18n.md), see [[generator-ignore-i18n-layout]]).
- Adding a language: copy the default files, translate, update the language selector. Users switch language at runtime through i18n `changeLanguage` and a registered language dropdown ([adding languages](../../agent-docs/frontend/i18n.md)).
- Labels set on the UI model elements become the generated translation keys, so Hungarian can also be provided in the m

[3050] TOOLRESULT: 
Operations that accept an uploaded file or return a generated one (CSV, JSON, DOCX, templates, credentials) go through the FileStore instead of passing bytes through the operation signature. The client holds a file reference; the backend reads and writes content by id.

## Practice

### Platform base: binary members and the token flow

In the model a file is a binary-typed member (binary types carry a MIME type and a maximum size; see [JSL language notes](../../agent-docs/model/jsl-language-notes.md)). An operation that returns a file wraps the binary in a transient member of an unmapped transfer object ([wrapper transfer object for binary outputs](../../agent-docs/model/advanced-modeling-patterns.md)). Over REST the client uses a two-phase token flow: it requests `<attributePath>/~upload-token`, posts the multipart data with the `X-Token` header, and downloads with `?disposition=inline|attachment` ([upload token flow](../../agent-docs/backend/platform-behavior-reference.md)). The generated frontend `fileHandling` helpers 

The backend storage is selected by environment variables (`JUDO_PLATFORM_FILESTORE` with `filesystem`, plus `JUDO_PLATFORM_FILESTORE_DIRECTORY` for the directory; see [application config](../../agent-docs/deployment/application-config.md)). The agent-docs also describe an S3 backend whose access URL is an internal `<protocol>:<fileId>-<filename>` URL, so downloads flow through the Karaf servlet ([filestore S3 details](../../agent-docs/deployment/osgi-runt

import InputAdornment from '@mui/material/InputAdornment';
import TextField from '@mui/material/TextField';
import { type ChangeEvent, type FC, useState } from 'react';
import { useTranslation } from 'react-i18next';

// One price format for the whole shop: two decimals plus currency, e.g. "79.90 EUR".
export const PRICE_CURRENCY = 'EUR';

export function formatPrice(value?: number | string | null): string {
  if (value === null || value === undefined || value === '') {
    return '';
  }
  const n = typeof value === 'number' ? value : Number(value);
  if (Number.isNaN(n)) {
    return '';
  }
  return `${n.toFixed(2)} ${PRICE_CURRENCY}`;
}

// Read-only display (used by cards and any custom page).
export const Price: FC<{ value?: number | null }> = ({ value }) => (
  <span data-testid="price">{formatPrice(value)}</span>
);

type PriceFieldProps = {
  data: { price?: number | null; __updateable?: boolean };
  validation: Map<string, string>;
  editMode?: boolean;
  isLoading?: boolean;
  storeDiff: (attributeName: any, value: any) => void;
  required?: boolean;
};

import { useEffect } from 'react';
import { useConfig } from '~/hooks';
import { useLayoutHelper } from '~/utilities/layout-helper';

// The generated layout stores "mini drawer" in the browser and only changes it when the window crosses a size
// tier. A window that was once small therefore opens collapsed (icons only, truncated labels, tiny logo) on a
// desktop screen. This component has no visuals: on laptop and wide screens it opens the drawer when the page
// loads and whenever the size tier changes. Registered as the documented AppBar extra component
// (agent-docs/frontend/hooks/ui-hooks.md); see GAPS.md R8-1.
export const DrawerExpander = () => {
  const { onChangeMiniDrawer } = useConfig();
  const { size } = useLayoutHelper();
  useEffect(() => {
    if (size === 'lg' || size === 'xl') {
      onChangeMiniDrawer(false);
    }
  }, [size]);
  return null;
};

[3226] ASSISTANT: ## Round 10 report: two fixes and Level 5 (F33–F40)

Everything is built and committed locally as `5131c2b`. The last full runs:
- **Admin:** 31 passed.
- **Customer:** 39 passed, 1 skipped (the old empty-state test) in the full run.
- **Re-run:** 3 level4 tests failed in that run because of a bug in my own test edit (a cleanup block pasted into the wrong tests). I fixed it and re-ran the whole `level4` file alone: 7 passed. I did not re-run the full customer suite after that fix.

### The two fixes
1. **History sorted oldest first.**
   - **Fix:** I set `sort=ASC` on the history column `happenedAt`, documented in tables-navigation.md and ui.md.
   - **Test:** The history rows come in the order "Order placed", "New → Paid", "Paid → Shipped", "Shipped → Delivered".
2. **"Ordered" read-only.** This took two tries.
   - **First try:** `enabledBy` a derived `false` flag greyed the field, but the date-picker icon stayed. The kb has no read-only switch for a mapped timestamp field.
   - **Fix:** A derived text `orderedAtLabel` ("2026-11-12 01:27") now replaces the field on both order views. The tables keep the real timestamp for sorting.
   - **Test:** A read-only text field, no date group, no picker button.

### Level 5, built
| ID | What was built |
|---|---|
| F33 | `ShopConfig` entity with shop name, free-shipping threshold, shipping fee, low-stock threshold and default VAT. Admin menu "Shop settings" (create and update, no delete). The shop home shows a banner with the shop name and free-shipping threshold. |
| F34 | VAT rate per category, or the default. Prices include VAT, so VAT = gross × rate / (100 + rate) and net = goods − discount − VAT. The cart and the order show goods, discount, net, VAT, shipping and total. The order keeps the amounts and the per-line VAT rate. |
| F35 | Flat shipping fee below the threshold, free from it. The test changes the threshold to exactly the cart value and the fee becomes "Free". |
| F36 | Admin menu "Coupons". Customers can "Apply coupon" and "Remove coupon" in the cart. Five distinct refusals: unknown, expired, not yet valid, used up, below minimum. The code is checked again at checkout and a use is counted there. |
| F37 | Sale price with a date window on the product. Cards show the sale price, the struck-through regular price and a "Sale" chip. The cart and the order use the sale price only while the window contains today. |
| F38 | Admin menu "Low stock": a derived access filtered by `stock < threshold` from the settings. Changing the threshold changes the list. |
| F39 | "Invoice" on paid, shipped and delivered orders, for the customer and the admin. The PDF opens inline in a dialog. Test checks that the response is `application/pdf`, that the content streams contain the order number and the total, and that the iframe appears. |
| F40 | EN/HU switch in the header of both actors. The shared Price component formats with `Intl` and shows `79,90 EUR` in Hungarian. Menus, buttons, cart labels and about 190 generated labels are translated. |
